A.9.2    Enumerating degrees of neighbors
In this section, we describe the procedure to enumerate degrees of neighbors of the center, described in
Section 11.2.2. The main routine is the enumPossibleBadWheels routine in Algorithm A.9.7. We use
the array CARTWHEEL DEGREES = [5,6,7,8,9], which represents the possible degrees assigned to vertices
except the center for free cartwheels with limited degrees. In the enumWheels routine in Algorithm A.9.5,
given a center degree d, we enumerate all degree assignments for neighbors up to rotational equivalence.
To achieve this, we generate all arrays of length d whose elements are chosen from CARTWHEEL DEGREES,
and retain only those for which every rotation is lexicographically greater than or equal to the array itself.
Considering this condition, we slightly prune the search as follows: for each d0 in CARTWHEEL DEGREES, we
first assign d0 to degreesr0s and do not assign any element smaller than it to the remaining positions in
the array. This is justified because no array containing an element strictly smaller than its first element
satisfies the above condition.
    Then, we call the generateCartwheel subroutine in Algorithm A.9.6 to generate a free cartwheel
with limited degrees with all the second neighbors having tail ranges r5, 9s.




                                                     76
Algorithm A.9.5 enumWheels(d)
Input: A degree of the center d.
Output: A set of free cartwheels with limited degrees and tail ranges pZ, δ ´ , δ ` q such that the center has
    degree d, the degrees of its neighbors are chosen from CARTWHEEL DEGREES, and all the degree-ranges
    of its second neighbors are r5, 9s.
 1: W Ð H
 2: procedure enumDegree(degrees, i, ilowest )
 3:     if i “ d then
 4:         if some rotation of degrees is lexicographically strictly smaller than degrees then
 5:             return
 6:         end if
 7:         pZ, δ ´ , δ ` q Ð generateCartWheel(d, degrees)
 8:         W Ð W Y tpZ, δ ´ , δ ` qu
 9:         return
10:     end if
11:     for all j P tilowest , . . . , 4u do
12:         degreesris Ð CARTWHEEL DEGREESrjs
13:         enumDegree(degrees, i ` 1, ilowest )
14:     end for
15:     return
16: end procedure
17: degrees Ð an array of size d.
18: for all j P t0, . . . , 4u do
19:     degrees[0] Ð CARTWHEEL DEGREESrjs
20:     enumDegree(degrees, 1, j)
21: end for
22: return W


    In the generateCartwheel subroutine in Algorithm A.9.6, we construct rotations representing the
rotation of vertices around each vertex. When rotationsris is a list of integers ra0 , . . . , ak s, it means
that vi is adjacent to va0 , . . . , vak in this rotation. If ai “ ´1, it represents the boundary.
    First, we compute the rotations for the vertices within the neighbors of the center. Second, we add
the second neighbors of the center, if they are adjacent to the neighbor of the center of degree not 9.
More precisely, for each neighbor vi of the center v, we add neighbors of it so that vi has a specified
degree, if this degree is not 9. Every time we add new vertices, we extend rotations. Finally, we
add ´1 to all second neighbors and to neighbors of degree 9 to represent the boundary. Then, we
call the fromVRotations subroutine in Algorithm A.5.1 to get the pseudo triangulation represented by
rotations.

Algorithm A.9.6 generateCartwheel(d, degrees)
Input: The degree of the center d, and an array degrees specifying the degrees of its neighbors.
Output: A free cartwheel with limited degrees and tail ranges C such that the center has degree d, the
    degrees of its neighbors are specified by degrees, and all the degree-ranges of its second neighbors
    are r5, 9s.
 1: rotations Ð a list of d ` 1 lists.
 2: rotationsr0s Ð r1, . . . , ds

 5:      i2 Ð i ´ 1 if i ą 1 otherwise d

 7: end for
 8: k Ð d ` 1                                                                  Ź k is the next vertex id to assign.
 9: for all i P t1, . . . , du do
10:      if degreesri ´ 1s “ 9 then                         Ź Do not add second neighbors if the degree is 9.
11:            continue
12:      end if
13:      A Ð degreesri ´ 1s ´ |rotationsris|                            Ź Number of new neighbors to add to vi
14:      repeat A times
15:            ilast Ð the last element of rotationsris
16:            rotations.push backprsq
17:            rotationsrks Ð ri, ilast s
18:            rotationsris.push backpkq
19:            rotationsrilast s Ð rks } rotationsrilast s
20:            k Ðk`1
21:      end
22:      ifirst Ð the first element of rotationsris
23:      ilast Ð the last element of rotationsris
24:      rotationsrifirst s.push backpilast q
25:      rotationsrilast s Ð rifirst s } rotationsrilast s
26: end for
27: for all i P t1, . . . , k ´ 1u do
28:      if i ą d or degreesri ´ 1s “ 9 then
29:            rotationsris.push backp´1q                                            Ź ´1 represents the boundary
30:      end if
31: end for
32: Z Ð fromVRotations(k, rotations)                                                      Ź V pZq “ tv0 , . . . , vk´1 u
33: δ ´ , δ ` : V pZq Ñ N such that δ ´ pv0 q “ δ ` pv0 q “ d, δ ´ pvi q “ δ ` pvi q “ degreesri ´ 1s if 0 ă i ď d,
    and δ ´ pvi q “ 5, δ ` pvi q “ 9 if d ă i ă k.
34: return pZ, δ ´ , δ ` q


    In the enumPossibleBadWheels routine, we call the enumWheels subroutine to obtain all combinations
of degrees for neighbors of the center. Then, we remove free cartwheels with limited degrees and tail
ranges if (i) an upper bound of the final charge is less than 0, or (ii) blocked by reducible configurations
from D s by calling the prune routine in Algorithm A.9.11.

A.9.4     Pruning

routine in Algorithm A.9.11 is the main routine that checks whether a given free cartwheel with limited

whether a rule in RzRj always applies to vj v for each 1 ď j ď i. Note that for each free combination
Rj˚ , we computed the flag fj such that fj pRq “ true if and only if R P Rj in the combineRules routine
in Algorithm A.8.2. Thus, we can easily check R P RzRj . The upperBoundOfCharge subroutine in


                                                              80
Algorithm A.9.13 calculates the upper bound of the final charge of the center of a given free cartwheel
with limited degrees and tail ranges, guaranteed by Lemma 11.6.

A.9.5    Refinement
In this section, we present the pseudocode regarding the refinement procedure, described in Section
11.2.4. The main routine for constructing C from Cd is given in Algorithm A.9.14. First, this routine
pushes each element in Cd into the queue. While the queue is not empty, we pop one element from the
queue. Then, we iterate through every rule R in R and every neighbor vi . We use the shouldRefine
subroutine in Algorithm A.9.16 to check whether we should refine based on whether R always or never
applies to vvi . If shouldRefine subroutine returns true, we assign refined flag to true, and do
the refinement procedure in the refinement subroutine in Algorithm A.9.17. For each refined free
cartwheel with limited degrees and tail ranges, we call the prune subroutine to check whether it is cut.
If refined flag is false at the end of iterations of all pairs of rules and neighbors, no refinement is
performed. In this case, we add it to the resulting set C.




                                                         82
Algorithm A.9.14 fixOutRules(Cd , R, R˚´D , s
                                           D)
Input: A set of free cartwheels with limited degrees and tail ranges Cd , the set of rules R, the set of
    combined rules R˚´D , and the set of reducible configurations s                D.
Output: The set of free cartwheels with limited degrees and tail ranges C, obtained by refining the
    cartwheels in Cd based on whether a rule in R applies from the center.
 1: Q Ð an empty queue
                     ´ `
 2: for all ppZC , δC , δC q, tRj˚ udj“1 q P Cd do
                          ´ `

    The refinement routine in Algorithm A.9.17 refines pZC , δC , δC q into Calways and Cnever depending on
whether R always/never applies to vvi . First, we calculate the homomorphism ϕ from ZR to ZC with
eR mapping to vvi . Then, we construct the set of vertices UR in ZR . Then, we refine degrees-ranges
of U “ ϕpUR q in the refineAlways and refineNever subroutines. The refineAlways subroutine in
Algorithm A.9.18 generates Calways , while the refineNever subroutine in Algorithm A.9.19 generates
Cnever . The algorithms for generating them are the same as those described in Section 11.2.4.

A.9.6     Overall algorithm
The overall algorithm for constructing Call to enumerate all bad cartwheels described in Section 11.2
is presented in the enumAllBadCartwheels routine in Algorithm A.9.20. The enumAllBadCartwheels
routine is not directly implemented as a C++function, but is implemented as a shell script. When
generating C0 and Call in the enumAllBadCartwheels routine, we execute the algorithm in parallel.
Note that the parallel updates to C0 and Call in the enumAllBadCartwheels routine do not cause race
conditions. This is because, in practice, C0 and Call are handled as directories, and each process simply
writes its output cartwheel to a uniquely named file within them. The enumAllBadCartwheels routine
calls the enumBadCartwheels subroutine in Algorithm A.9.21 to enumerate bad cartwheels generated
                                    ´     `
from a given singleton set tpZC0 , δC0
                                       , δC0
                                             qu.

A.10      Free combinations of free cartwheels
In this section, we provide the pseudocodes corresponding to the algorithms described in Section 11.3.
    We use the deleteDegreeFromKto9 routine to convert the set of free cartwheels Call . To prove Lemma
8.3, we convert Call by calling deleteDegreeFromKto9 with k “ 9. Furthermore, to prove Lemma 8.5,
we convert the resulting set by calling deleteDegreeFromKto9 with k “ 8.
                                               ´ `                                                        ´
    Note that, for every free cartwheel pZC , δC , δC q in Call , the center v has a fixed degree (i.e., δC pvq “
 `
δC pvq). Each neighbor of the center also has a fixed degree.

Algorithm A.10.1 deleteDegreeFromKTo9(C, k)
Input: A set of free cartwheels C, an positive integer k ď 9.
Output: The set of free cartwheels obtained from C by deleting free cartwheels containing vertices of
    fixed degree k and updating degree range rk ´ 1, 9s to fixed degree k ´ 1.
 1: C 1 Ð H
                    ´ `

 4:     for all v P V pZC q do
                ´         `

 6:             remove Ð true
 7:             break
                      ´                   `
 8:         else if δC  pvq “ k ´ 1 and δC  pvq “ 9 then
                 `

10:         end if
11:     end for
12:     if remove “ false then
                                ´ `
13:         C 1 Ð C 1 Y tpZC , δC , δC qu
14:     end if
15: end for

A.10.1     A vertex of degree 8
The main routine to check Lemma 8.3 is checkDeg8 in Algorithm A.10.4. As explained in Section 11.3,
we have a lemma below. Recall that in Lemma 8.3 (iii), we require that two neighbors of degree 7 are as
close as possible in the successor order. Thus, in the check787 routine in Algorithm A.10.7, we calculate
the minimum distance of the successor order, and only check pairs of neighbors satisfying this minimum
distance.
